VERDICT: refine

The build is genuinely high-quality and source-complete — every mechanism I traced is
correctly implemented and matches both the spec and its own documented intent, with no
defenses-as-words and no missing component categories. I found **zero actual code defects**.
But I cannot certify convergence: the overwhelming majority of the build's load-bearing
behavior has never been empirically verified by anyone, and the one verification class I am
required to exercise live (the CLI) could not be run. The findings below are about
*demonstrated robustness / evidence*, not architecture (the spec is sound) and not broken
code. This is `refine`, not `operator_escalate`.

CONFIRMED_PASSING. Spot-read PARTIAL reasons for idx 1, 42, 71 — all share the shape
"mechanism confirmed from source; actual runtime behavior requires Docker."

- Playwright on the served board.html (ui class): page renders, correct shell + design tokens,

### Findings emitted by this reviewer

#### [G1] Whole-build runtime / integration behavior is unverified — the blocking reason for non-convergence — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** The entire live surface — all 21 challenge end-to-end chains + MISSION-ALPHA + bonus,
  the 4 victim-bot cycles, Docker orchestration (garden start/status/reset lifecycle), nginx routing,
  PostgreSQL/Redis seeding, Redis sessions, and the BL-02 coupon race. Recorded as 173 PARTIAL in
  ai_verdicts.jsonl; admitted as "~229 fully unverified (Docker-blocked) + ~15 logic-only" in
  docs/verification-matrix.md.
  **What:** No end-to-end behavior has been empirically confirmed by anyone. The contractor's own
  position is explicit (verification-matrix.md): "all Tier 3 features are expected to pass — the code
  has been read and verified correct by static analysis; the only remaining gap is empirical
  execution." That is reading-as-verification. I reached the same favorable read of the same code —
  which is precisely the same-model-bias trap: a fresh reviewer reading the same source reaches the
  same wrong conclusions as the author if there is a runtime break. For a CTF-style l
```

#### [G2] Two contractor verification docs contradict each other and one materially understates the gap — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** UNVERIFIED.md vs docs/verification-matrix.md.
  **What:** UNVERIFIED.md states "the features below [are] unverifiable" and then lists **only feature
  126**, implying everything else is verified. verification-matrix.md says **~229 fully unverified +
  ~15 logic-only**, and ai_verdicts.jsonl shows **173 PARTIAL**. These cannot all be true. UNVERIFIED.md
  also addresses the harness directly — "The harness does not lower its own guardrails to verify —
  remediation is operator-side" — framing the unverified count as not-the-build's-problem. Per this
  role's rules, harness/reviewer-addressed framing that argues unverified ≠ failing is a finding;
  combined with the 1-vs-229 understatement it is, at minimum, a doc-integrity defect that could
  mislead an operator into shipping an unverified lab.
  **Why:** An operator reading UNVERIFIED.md would believe the lab is ~99% verified when ~63% of
  features (per the empirical record) have never run. Misrepresented verification state 
```

#### [G3] The lowest-cost real verification (offline jest) was never captured — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** node-app/tests/*.test.js (waf.test.js / jwt.test.js / auth-middleware.test.js), run via
  jest + supertest + pg-mem + ioredis-mock per verification-matrix.md.
  **What:** These tests exercise the **real** Express handlers/middleware (waf.test.js: "tests real JS,
  not a Python mirror") and, per the matrix, need **no Docker** (in-process Postgres/Redis emulation).
  Yet the corresponding features (AUTH-01/02, BL-01/03, WAF, INJ-03, staffSessionProof) are still
  marked PARTIAL "requires Docker" in ai_verdicts.jsonl — i.e. the strongest Docker-free evidence the
  build already ships was never run/recorded. (I could not run it myself: node is denied to reviewers.)
  **Why:** Several PARTIAL HTTP-layer features could become genuinely confirmed at near-zero cost,
  materially raising real coverage and de-risking G1 for the auth/business-logic tracks.
  **Fix shape:** Run `cd node-app && npm test`, capture the pass output as evidence, and reflect it in
  the verification docs / fe
```

#### [G4] AUTH-02 end-to-end solvability rests on byte-exact PEM reconstruction — never tested e2e — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** node-app/src/utils/jwt.js verifyAlgFlexible (lines 142-160) — HMAC secret is
  `Buffer.from(rsaPublicKeyPem)`, with a LF-normalization step (line 36) acknowledged as fragile.
  **What:** The alg-confusion exploit only works if the solver's PEM (reconstructed from the JWKS n/e)
  is **byte-identical** to the server's in-memory `rsaPublicKeyPem` (line endings, trailing newline,
  header wrapping). The code comments flag this risk but it has never been exercised end-to-end, so
  whether a standard JWKS→PEM toolchain produces a matching byte string for a flagship challenge is
  unconfirmed.
  **Why:** If the reconstruction does not byte-match, AUTH-02 is unsolvable despite correct-looking
  code — a silent dead challenge. This is the highest-fragility spot among the mechanisms I traced.
  **Fix shape:** During the G1 live run, solve AUTH-02 with a real off-the-shelf solver path
  (jwks→PEM→HS256 sign) and confirm acceptance at /api/manager/promotions; if it fails, normalize the

```

VERDICT: refine

Whole-build gap/robustness review of "Juice Garden" — a Docker-based moderate-difficulty
vulnerable web lab (Node/Express target app, Flask challenge board, victim-bot, nginx WAF,
Postgres/Redis, `garden` CLI). The architecture is sound and most of the Node app's
per-challenge exploit logic is live-confirmed working. But one core challenge is
confirmed broken, and a large fraction of the build (6 of 21 challenges, the entire
operator CLI/orchestration layer, and the dynamic challenge-board UI) has never been
exercised end-to-end — so the build is not yet solid against the spec.

## Claim enumeration — what I actually checked

  BAC-01/02/03/04 (66,67,136,139,174,190,202,203,205,215), INJ-01/03/04
  (55,60,61,62,63,129,175,191,204), BL-01/02/03 (82,83,85,86,140,194,197). Those tracks are solid.

### Findings emitted by this reviewer

#### [G1] INJ-02 order-search endpoint is structurally broken — challenge unsolvable AND endpoint 500s on every input — severity: critical

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `node-app/src/routes/orders.js:225` — `POST /api/orders/search`. The column type is set in `node-app/src/models/index.js:54-56` (`status: DataTypes.ENUM('pending','confirmed','shipped','delivered','refunded')`).
  **What:** The raw SQL is `... WHERE user_id = '${req.user.id}' AND status LIKE '%${searchQuery}%'`. `status` is a PostgreSQL ENUM, and `enum ~~ text` (LIKE) has no implicit cast, so PostgreSQL raises `operator does not exist: enum_orders_status ~~ unknown` and the route returns HTTP 500 for *any* query — benign ("confirmed") or malicious. The UNION-bypass payload never executes because the first SELECT's WHERE fails at plan time. The evaluator confirmed this LIVE (ai_verdicts idx 56/57/58: "operator does not exist: enum_orders_status ~~ unknown … claimed flag retrieval is IMPOSSIBLE … Docker doesn't fix the enum type bug"), and I confirmed it by reading the model + route.
  **Why:** INJ-02 is one of the 21 advertised challenges; it cannot be solved end-to-end, dire
```

#### [G2] Six of the 21 challenges (CS-01, CS-02, CS-03, SS-01, SS-02, SS-03) are source-confirmed only — never run end-to-end — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** ai_verdicts idx 71/72/73/132/256 (CS-01), 74/75/219/238/257 (CS-02), 77/78/173/241/258 (CS-03), 79/201/233/266 (SS-01), 80/214/260 (SS-02), 81/165/261 (SS-03) — all PARTIAL with reasons of the form "mechanism confirmed from source … actual exploitation requires Docker/browser/bot."
  **What:** These are the build's most moving-part-heavy challenges (WAF allowlist + nonce-CSP + JSONP + non-HttpOnly cookie + manager_bot/admin_bot cycles for CS-01; cross-origin postMessage + iframe for CS-02; SameSite Lax/Strict boundary across attacker.lab for CS-03; SSRF→redis-commander key read for SS-01; regex-simulated file:// XXE for SS-02; double-extension upload→EJS RCE for SS-03). Code review shows them structurally plausible (victim-bot delivers the admin cookie; ss01 flag is seeded into Redis at `server.js:139`; the SS-02 entity regex reads the flag file), but none has been demonstrated to actually yield its flag.
  **Why:** INJ-02 is the proof that "source-confirmed, requires Docker
```

#### [G3] The `garden` CLI and the Docker orchestration / deployability layer are entirely unverified — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `garden/cli.py` (start/stop/status/reset/restart/hosts/flags); `docker-compose.yml`; features 1,2,9,10,12,13,14,16,17,176,200,250,272,273,274,275 — all PARTIAL ("requires Docker").
  **What:** `garden start` bringing up a healthy 8-service stack, `garden status` health reporting, soft/hard `reset` regenerating 23 flags, network egress-blocking (`internal:true` on app-/platform-internal), and redis-commander NOT being host-published have never been run. The CLI isn't even installed in this review environment and no stack is reachable.
  **Why:** This is the operator's entire interface and the lab's containment story. If `garden start` does not produce a healthy stack (or if egress isn't actually blocked / redis-commander is reachable from the host), the lab is unusable or unsafe regardless of how good the per-challenge code is. "All criteria on a fresh start" (feature 250/272) is the top-level acceptance gate and is unproven.
  **Fix shape:** On a host with Docker, run `pip i
```

#### [G4] The dynamic challenge-board UI is unverified — board.html is an unrendered template in every available environment — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `flask-platform/src/templates/board.html`; features 91,92,93,95,96,158,179,185,220,221,222,224,237,249 (PARTIAL). My Playwright snapshot shows literal `{% for challenge in challenges %}` / `{{ challenge.code }}` and a single stub card.
  **What:** Card rendering, track/difficulty/solved filtering, hint purchase tiers, flag submission (correct→points/solution-notes, wrong→shake), scoreboard live update, solve confetti/toast, hidden-bonus exclusion — none can be exercised without the Flask backend + SQLite. Static structure, CSS palette, fonts, and a11y scaffolding ARE confirmed (and Flask TestClient confirmed some backend logic per idx 94/95), but the rendered user journeys are not.
  **Why:** The challenge board is the practitioner's primary UI; "the whole journey must work" (select card → open slide-over → submit flag → see solve state) is a spec promise that is currently only half-shown (open/close mechanics confirmed, content rendering not).
  **Fix shape:** Run the Flask
```

#### [G5] bcrypt cost factor deviates from spec example — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `node-app/src/routes/auth.js` (per ai_verdicts idx 186: cost = 8).
  **What:** Spec says "deliberately low (e.g. 4 or 6)"; the build uses 8.
  **Why:** Still below production defaults so AUTH-04 brute-force with the 500-entry wordlist remains feasible, but slower than the spec intended and unverified for timing.
  **Fix shape:** Either lower to 6 to match the spec's intent, or confirm by timing that a 500-word brute force completes acceptably at cost 8 and note the deviation.

-
```

#### [G6] Exploit-server CORS is broader than the spec describes — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `flask-platform` exploit_server collect endpoint (ai_verdicts idx 105): GET/POST responses return `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`.
  **What:** Spec frames the collect CORS as "permits cross-origin POST from http://garden.lab:8081"; the implementation allows any origin via wildcard.
  **Why:** Functionally permits the intended CS-02 exfil (8081 ⊂ *), so not exploit-breaking, but it is looser than the documented scope.
  **Fix shape:** Tighten to the documented origin if the narrower scope is intended, or update the spec text to reflect the wildcard if intentional.
```

LIVE EMPIRICAL evidence (`ai_verdicts.jsonl` idx 79, 201, 233, 266) confirms `/apiv2/keys` and `/apiv2/key/get?key=` return **HTTP 404** in the deployed `rediscommander/redis-commander:latest`. The flag is only reachable via the *undocumented* `/apiv2/keystree/R:redis:6379:0` (key list) and `/apiv2/key/R:redis:6379:0/garden:ss01_flag` (value). Tellingly, the node-app code already knows the correct path — `node-app/src/routes/webhooks.js:34` comments the target as `…/apiv2/key/R:redis:6379:0/garden:ss01_flag` — but that fix was never propagated to the practitioner-facing Flask hints.


#### [G2] SS-03 documented EJS payload uses bare `require`, which is not in scope under EJS 3.1.10 — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `flask-platform/src/app.py:336-337` — `hint_t3`/`solution_notes` use `<%= require('fs').readFileSync('/app/flags/ss03.txt','utf8') %>`. Renderer: `node-app/src/routes/products.js:256` (`ejs.render(content, {})`); `node-app/package.json` pins `ejs ^3.1.10`.
  **What:** EJS 3.x compiles templates via `new Function(...)`, whose body runs in global scope where **`require` is undefined** (CommonJS `require` is module-scoped) → `ReferenceError: require is not defined`, caught by the preview's try/catch (products.js:263-266) and returned as an error with no flag. The build's own INJ-03 RCE gadget for the *same* EJS engine deliberately uses the working form `global.process.mainModule.require('child_process')…` (`node-app/src/routes/users.js:96,107`), and INJ-03 is LIVE-confirmed (idx 60/61). SS-03's bare-`require` payload was never live-tested (idx 81/155/261 are all PARTIAL), so this latent break went uncaught.
  **Why:** SS-03 is **unsolvable using the published payload** even tho
```

#### [G3] SS-02 "XXE" is a hand-rolled regex simulation, not real external-entity processing — fragile to payload variants — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `node-app/src/routes/products.js:281-306` (`ENTITY_RE = /<!ENTITY\s+(\w+)\s+SYSTEM\s+["'](file:\/\/[^"']+)["']/g` + `fs.readFileSync`); `xml2js.parseStringPromise` at L308 does no entity expansion.
  **What:** Only `<!ENTITY name SYSTEM "file://…">` (single/double-quoted) is resolved; PUBLIC-id entities, parameter entities (`%`), alternate whitespace/no-quote forms, or any entity the regex doesn't match silently fail. The spec permits "LIBXML_NOENT or equivalent" and the one documented payload (idx 80/260) works, so this isn't blocking — but for a "moderate-difficulty" XXE lab the vuln surface is narrower/more brittle than a real parser, and a practitioner using a valid-but-unmatched XXE shape gets nothing.
  **Why:** Robustness/pedagogical-fidelity gap on feature idx 214/80; not a functional break for the canonical payload.
  **Fix shape:** Either broaden the entity matcher to cover the common valid shapes, or (better) use an XML parser configured to actually resolve `file:
```


#### [G5] `docker-compose.yml` declares obsolete `version` key — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `docker-compose.yml:1` (`version: "3.9"`).
```

```
  `SELECT id, status::text, total_price_cents, created_at FROM orders
   WHERE user_id = '${req.user.id}' AND status::text LIKE '%${searchQuery}%'`.
- What: The prior live-empirical defect (idx 56/57/58, flagged "IMPOSSIBLE": `status LIKE`
  on a Postgres enum threw HTTP 500) was patched by casting `status::text`. But (a) this
  fix postdates the last evaluation and was never re-run live; (b) feature_list_digest
  still records passes:false and ai_verdicts still records PARTIAL for these features;
  (c) the intended UNION-based exfil must now match 4 columns whose first is `id` UUID —
  a `UNION SELECT` of text/other types against a UUID column will itself throw a type
  error unless the payload casts correctly, which is untested.
- Why it matters: A headline INJ challenge currently stands as "fails" in the build's own
  truth-records, and the candidate fix has a plausible second failure mode (UUID column
  type-matching) that
```

#### [G2] (major) CS-01 / CS-02 / CS-03 victim-bot exploitation chains are unconfirmed — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
end-to-end.
- Where: victim-bot/src/bot.js (manager approves reviews → admin visits /reviews and
  drains the URL queue; non-HttpOnly admin cookie), flask-platform exploit_server.py
  (save→serve cross-origin garden.lab:8081 / cross-site attacker.lab; /collect CORS *),
  nginx routing for :8081 and attacker.lab.
- What: Each mechanism is individually source-faithful, but the full stored-XSS →
  bot-execution → exfil-to-/collect loop (and the CS-02 same-site vs CS-03 cross-site
  SameSite distinction) is only PARTIAL in ai_verdicts; I could not drive the bot loop in
  this sandbox.
- Why it matters: The bot-driven client-side chain is the lab's headline differentiator
  over a static CTF; "the whole journey must work," and timing/cookie/SameSite/queue
  details are precisely where these chains silently break.
- Fix: On the live stack, plant each CS payload, let the victim-bot cycle, and confirm the
  flag lands at the exploit-server /collect endpoint for all three (note CS-02 must
  suc
```

#### [G3] (minor) redis-commander pinned to `:latest` while SS-01 hints hardcode a — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
version-specific API path.
- Where: docker-compose.yml `image: rediscommander/redis-commander:latest`; SS-01 hints
  in flask-platform app.py and webhooks.js comment reference `/apiv2/keystree/...` and
  `/apiv2/key/R:redis:6379:0/garden:ss01_flag`.
- What: The SS-01 solve depends on redis-commander exposing that exact `/apiv2` route;
  `:latest` can drift and change/remove it, silently breaking the challenge on a future
  `docker compose pull`.
- Why it matters: Reproducibility of a flag-bearing challenge should not hinge on an
  unpinned upstream image — this was already an empirically-flagged path (idx 233).
- Fix: Pin redis-commander to the digest/tag whose `/apiv2` surface matches the hints.
```

#### [G4] (minor) Leftover scratch file in the app tree. — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Where: node-app/.scratch_ejs_test.js.
- What: Dev scratch artifact shipped in the build.
- Why it matters: Cleanliness; a stray dotfile in the vulnerable app can confuse solvers
  reading source for hints.
- Fix: Remove it.
```

VERDICT: refine

# F2 Build Review — Juice Garden Vulnerable Web Lab

Whole-build gap/robustness review. Architecture is sound (8-service compose stack, node target + Flask board + victim-bot + flag-init, dual isolated networks, WAF-lite, JWT verifier separation, staffSessionProof gate). This is NOT a blueprint problem — the findings below are localized implementation bugs. Two are deterministic challenge-solve failures, which per the spec's DETERMINISTIC FAILURE EXCEPTION block the "deployable" verdict and force `refine`.

Verification was performed live: API/UI driven through Playwright against http://127.0.0.1:80 (browser netns), plus source reading. The `garden` CLI entrypoint was exercised via Bash (`garden --help`).

---

## Findings (gaps to close)

[G1] SS-03 intended exploit path is dead — image-preview route is shadowed (CRITICAL)
Where: node-app/src/routes/products.js — `GET /:id` registered at line 98, `GET /image-preview` registered at line 232 (after it).
What: `GET /api/products/image-preview?file=...` never reaches the EJS handler. Express matches the earlier single-segment route `/:id` with `:id="image-preview"`, so `Product.findByPk("image-preview")` fails the INTEGER cast and returns `500 {"error":"Failed to fetch product"}`. Confirmed live with two clean probes (`?file=nonexistent.jpg` and no-param both return the `/:id` handler's exact 500 string; a reachable handler would return 404 "File not found" or 400 "file parameter required"). The app's OWN generated `preview_url` from image-upload points at this shadowed path. EJS rendering exists ONLY in image-preview, so there is no workaround — the SS-03 vuln (double-extension `.ejs.jpg` → `ejs.render()`) cannot fire. Fails criterion "SS-03 File Upload Double-Extension EJS Bypass" (spec 1509-1512: "Passes iff EJS executes and /app/flags/ss03.txt content appears in response").
Why: Route registration order — a parameterized catch-all (`/:id`) declared before a literal static route (`/image-preview`) o

# Build Review — Juice Garden (F2 whole-build gap/robustness review)

One critical, deterministic defect blocks deployability: **two challenges (CS-02 and CS-03) are
structurally unsolvable** on a healthy, fully-started stack because the victim-bot cannot reach the
exploit-server queue across the network partition. The rest of the build is largely solid — core app,
auth, WAF enforcement, the challenge board, and a representative non-bot challenge all work end-to-end
under live testing. The architecture is correct (this is NOT a blueprint problem); the fix is a
localized 2-line wiring change. Hence `refine`, not `operator_escalate` and not `converged`.

---

## Scope & method
- Read cold. Judged on merits. Did NOT re-decide architecture.
- Could not run Docker in this sandbox (no docker.sock). A live backend is reachable at
  http://127.0.0.1:80 (node-app at `/`, `/api/*`; flask board at `/platform/*`) and was used for

  not treat these as build defects.
- Verification classes satisfied:

    end-to-end flag capture and WAF enforcement checks.

---

The victim-bot's URL-queue consumer cannot reach the Flask exploit server, so submitted attack pages

VERDICT: refine

# Build Review — Juice Garden (vulnerable web lab platform)

Gap/robustness review of the whole build against app_spec.txt + user_prompt.txt.
Architecture is treated as settled (F1); this review lists gaps/weaknesses that keep
the build from delivering the spec fully and solidly. One critical finding is present,
so per the convergence rule the build cannot be `converged` this round.

Verification classes exercised in-session:
- cli: ran `garden status` and `garden --help` (real Click CLI; rendered status table + service URLs; rejected `--version`).
- ui: Playwright navigated the live stack at http://127.0.0.1/platform/ (title "Challenge Board — Juice Garden").

---

## CRITICAL

### [G1] victim-bot URL-queue dequeue targets an unreachable host → CS-02 & CS-03 unsolvable via the intended solo flow
- Where: victim-bot/src/bot.js:198, 211, 219 (function `processUrlQueue`, invoked at :181 inside the admin cycle); docker-compose.yml:157-158 (flask-platform → `platform-internal` only), :186-188 (victim-bot env `PLATFORM_URL: "http://nginx/platform"` + `networks: [app-internal]` only).
- What: `processUrlQueue()` fetches the pending queue and posts `mark-visited` against `http://flask-platform:5000/platform/exploit-server/queue/...`. The bot runs on `app-internal`; flask-platform is on `platform-internal`; the two share no network. Docker's embedded DNS is network-scoped, so `flask-platform` does not resolve/route from the bot. The axios call throws and is swallowed by the catch at bot.js:227-228 ("[bot-queue] Error fetching queue"), so the function silently returns and the URL queue is never drained.
- Why it matters: the exploit-server "Send to Bot" → bot visits the attacker URL → flag is exfiltrated to the collect table is THE delivery mechanism the spec advertises for the CS track. With the dequeue dead, CS-02 (postMessage DOM XSS, cross-origin/same-site) and CS-03 (cross-site CSRF/exfil) cannot be completed by a solo practitioner through the intended

### Findings emitted by this reviewer

#### [G1] victim-bot URL-queue dequeue targets an unreachable host → CS-02 & CS-03 unsolvable via the intended solo flow — severity: critical

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Where: victim-bot/src/bot.js:198, 211, 219 (function `processUrlQueue`, invoked at :181 inside the admin cycle); docker-compose.yml:157-158 (flask-platform → `platform-internal` only), :186-188 (victim-bot env `PLATFORM_URL: "http://nginx/platform"` + `networks: [app-internal]` only).
- What: `processUrlQueue()` fetches the pending queue and posts `mark-visited` against `http://flask-platform:5000/platform/exploit-server/queue/...`. The bot runs on `app-internal`; flask-platform is on `platform-internal`; the two share no network. Docker's embedded DNS is network-scoped, so `flask-platform` does not resolve/route from the bot. The axios call throws and is swallowed by the catch at bot.js:227-228 ("[bot-queue] Error fetching queue"), so the function silently returns and the URL queue is never drained.
- Why it matters: the exploit-server "Send to Bot" → bot visits the attacker URL → flag is exfiltrated to the collect table is THE delivery mechanism the spec advertises for the CS track
```

#### [G2] Scheduled victim-bot cycles (manager/admin/staff) were observed non-functional at runtime; root cause not isolated — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Where: victim-bot/src/bot.js — `managerBotCycle`/`adminBotCycle`/`staffBotCycle`, scheduled at :263-268; these drive node-app via the browser→nginx path (correct target, unlike G1).
- What: prior live evidence (ai_verdicts idx 6/71/123/273) reports submitted reviews were not auto-approved after waiting through a cycle, i.e. the manager/staff automation did not visibly act. On code-read the manager path (login → GET /reviews → POST /reviews/:id/approve) lines up with the node-app routes (manager.js:75-125), so I could not pin a defect from source alone, and I did not reproduce the cycle live in this review (requires authenticating as a normal user and waiting through 30-60s timers).
- Why it matters: CS-01 (stored XSS surfaced when a review is approved) and INJ-01 (second-order, surfaced to staff_bot) depend on these cycles firing. If they are unreliable, several track challenges degrade even though G1 is fixed.
- Fix shape: reproduce live (submit a review as a normal user, wait one m
```

#### [G3] (REFUTED — recorded so it is not re-chased) audit_log is NOT "never written" — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Prior evidence (ai_verdicts idx 195) claims `AuditLog.create()` is never called and the table is always empty. This is wrong. `AuditLog.create(...)` is invoked in three reachable handlers — node-app/src/routes/orders.js:181 (order.created), users.js:80 (user.profile_updated), products.js:194 (review.submitted) — the model schema (models/index.js:117-129, table `audit_log`, fields actor_id/action/target_type/target_id/details_json/ip_address/timestamp) matches every field written, and there is a reader at admin.js:63 (`GET /api/admin/audit`). No action needed; do not spend refinement effort here.

###
```

#### [G4] No CSRF tokens on HTML forms (spec realism gap) — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Where: node-app form rendering / routes (no token issuance or verification anywhere); corroborated by ai_verdicts idx 127 (CONFIRMED_FAILING).
- What: the spec calls for CSRF tokens on legitimate HTML forms as a baseline; none are present.
- Why it matters: low. It is a realism/spec-fidelity gap. It does not block any challenge (and is arguably even convenient for CS-03), but it diverges from what the spec promised.
- Fix shape: add a synchronizer token to legit forms + verify on state-changing POSTs, while leaving the CS-03 target endpoint exploitable per design (the intended lesson should be a specific missing/again-checked control, not a blanket absence).

###
```

#### [G5] Two SERIOUS axe accessibility violations on /platform/ — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Where: challenge-board UI; corroborated by ai_verdicts idx 157 (CONFIRMED_FAILING).
- What: (a) the slide-over detail modal lacks an accessible name (aria-dialog-name) — this contradicts feature 119 which claims the dialog is labelled; (b) color-contrast on the server_side track pill (#e86838 ≈ 4.41:1, below the 4.5:1 AA threshold).
- Why it matters: low; polish/quality gap, no challenge impact, but a stated feature (labelled dialog) is not actually met.
- Fix shape: add `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` to the slide-over; darken the server_side pill text/background to clear 4.5:1.

###
```

#### [G6] Invalid CSP source emits a console error on every page — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
- Where: node-app/src/routes/pages.js:32 and :73 — `script-src` includes the bare path `/api/products/search`, which is not a valid CSP host-source.
- What: every page load logs "Content-Security-Policy: Couldn't parse invalid host /api/products/search" (observed in the captured console log).
- Why it matters: low; cosmetic/correctness blemish. CS-01's JSONP bypass still works because `'self'` already covers the same-origin script src, so the invalid token is inert — but it is noise that can mislead a learner reading the CSP.
- Fix shape: drop the invalid token (rely on `'self'`), or if a specific endpoint must be whitelisted express it as a valid host-source.

---
```

# Build Review — Juice Garden (F2 whole-build gap/robustness review)

**Verdict: refine**

Verification class {cli, ui} satisfied: ran the `garden` CLI (Bash) and drove the live
stack via Playwright (navigate/snapshot/evaluate). Judged the build cold against
`app_spec.txt` + `user_prompt.txt`. The architecture (F1) is sound and is NOT in question;
the gaps below are local implementation/config defects, so this is `refine`, not
`operator_escalate`.

---

## Why not `converged`: the entire CS track is not solvable end-to-end

The victim-bot subsystem — which gates CS-01, CS-02, CS-03 and the INJ-01 second-order
auto-trigger — does not perform its scheduled actions in the delivered build. This is
confirmed three independent ways: (a) static config/code trace, (b) my own live tests on
the running stack, and (c) the build's **own** `UNVERIFIED.md`, which already admits the
behaviors are "confirmed non-functional."

### CRITICAL-1 — CS-02 / CS-03 URL queue is unreachable by construction (disjoint Docker networks)
- `docker-compose.yml`: `victim-bot` is on `app-internal` only (L187–188); `flask-platform`
  is on `platform-internal` only (L157–158). They share **no** network, both are
  `internal: true`, and there is no `docker-compose.override.yml`.
- `victim-bot/src/bot.js` `processUrlQueue()` hardcodes
  `http://flask-platform:5000/platform/exploit-server/queue/pending` (and `.../mark-visited`)
  at L198/L210/L218. Docker embedded DNS cannot resolve `flask-platform` from `app-internal`,
  so every `axios.get` falls into the catch block → the queue is **never** dequeued. The
  reachable `PLATFORM_URL=http://nginx/platform` (compose L186, bot.js L31) is defined but
  **not used** in the queue functions.
- LIVE: I saved a beacon payload via the exploit server, enqueued its URL (`queue_length:1`),
  then polled `/queue/pending` + `/collections` for 40s+ (> one 30s admin_bot cycle). Queue
  stayed at `1 pending`, beacon never fired, `0` collections.
- Build's own `UNVERIFIED.

### Findings emitted by this reviewer

#### [G1] Victim-bot URL queue never dequeues — bot can't reach flask-platform (wrong hostname + no shared network) — severity: critical

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** victim-bot/src/bot.js `processUrlQueue()` lines 198/211/219 (hardcoded `http://flask-platform:5000/...`); `PLATFORM_URL` defined line 31 / set to `http://nginx/platform` in docker-compose.yml line 188 but **never used in code**. Network assignments: victim-bot `networks: [app-internal]` (compose 189–190) vs flask-platform `networks: [platform-internal]` (compose 159–160) — no shared network; only nginx bridges both.
  **What:** The bot's only dequeue path calls `flask-platform:5000` directly. Docker embedded DNS resolves a service name only across a shared network; victim-bot and flask-platform share none, so the name doesn't resolve → `axios.get` throws → caught at bot.js:227 (`[bot-queue] Error fetching queue`) → no URL is ever visited. Empirically reproduced on the live stack: I submitted a queue entry at 22:48:37 and it was still `pending` at 22:54:24 — **~5.8 min, ~11 missed 30s cycles** (matches the evaluator's idx 273 "un-dequeued after 8 min"). nginx (compose 217–220
```

#### [G2] Victim-bot autonomous operation is unverified beyond source-review; every bot-dependent challenge was confirmed via manual simulation — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** ai_verdicts.jsonl idx 107/108/109 (staff/manager/admin scheduling) marked passing on "source review … Deterministic from source. No Docker needed"; idx 52 (INJ-01) via manual `POST /api/staff/report`; idx 71 (CS-01) via manual review-post + manual manager approval + manual admin_bot login. bot.js cycles depend on each `*BotCycle()` actually logging in and completing without exception.
  **What:** The build proves the bot *schedules* cycles (`setInterval` lines 266–268) but not that they *run to completion* against the live app. The single observable autonomous signal — the URL queue — is broken (G1), which is direct evidence at least part of the bot doesn't function end-to-end. Whether staff_bot's report (INJ-01) and manager_bot-approval→admin_bot-visit (CS-01) fire autonomously for a practitioner who lacks bot credentials is empirically unconfirmed.
  **Why:** INJ-01, CS-01, CS-02, CS-03 (4 of 21 challenges) all depend on the bot acting autonomously. A solo practitioner can
```

#### [G3] Queue processing is coupled to the /reviews visit in the same try-block — an active CS-01 payload can starve it even after G1 — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** victim-bot/src/bot.js `adminBotCycle()` lines 173–181: `reviewsPage.goto('/reviews', { waitUntil:'networkidle', timeout:20000 })` runs immediately before `processUrlQueue(context)` inside one try; any throw on the reviews navigation skips the queue for that cycle.
  **What:** `/reviews` is exactly where stored CS-01 XSS payloads live. A payload that keeps the page network-active (or any 20s timeout) makes the `goto` throw, so `processUrlQueue` never runs that cycle.
  **Why:** Reduces robustness of the (post-G1) queue under the very condition the lab is designed to create; intermittent CS-02/CS-03 starvation.
  **Fix shape:** Decouple queue processing from the reviews visit — own try/catch, or its own `setInterval` independent of `adminBotCycle`.

---

Core build is otherwise solid: CLI works, challenge board + exploit-server UI render and interact correctly with 0 console errors, and the WAF/JWT/vuln-app defenses are real, mechanically-enforced code paths verified live (not
```

  timingSafeEqual + expiry. Correct.

## Summary
The build is largely solid: the stack is live, the database is seeded, flags are minted, and a sampled set of non-bot challenges captures real flags end-to-end. However, the victim-bot — the engine behind the entire client-side track — is non-functional in the running stack. With ~20–25% of the challenge content unsolvable through its intended path, the build cannot converge. The defect is a functional/implementation bug within the existing architecture (not an architecture problem), so the correct verdict is `refine`, not `operator_escalate`.

## Verification performed (class coverage)
- **CLI class:** `garden --help` — entry point wired, prints usage + documented commands (start/status/reset/restart/stop/flags/hosts). Clean exit.
- **UI class:** Playwright `browser_navigate` to http://127.0.0.1/ (loads, title "Home — Garden Fresh"); `browser_evaluate` against the platform exploit-server queue; `browser_wait_for` observation window; `browser_snapshot`.
- **Sampled non-bot exploits confirmed WORKING (prior session, via authenticated API/Playwright):** BL-01 negative-quantity → `debug_flag`; BL-03 Int32 overflow → `loyalty_payout.flag`; INJ-02 `UNION/**/SELECT` WAF bypass → flag row; INJ-03 EJS SSTI → `7*7=49` rendered; BAC-02 mass-assignment → role elevated to `staff`. The non-bot surface is solid.

## Gaps

[G1] CRITICAL — victim-bot does not process the exploit queue (entire client-side track dead)
- Where: `victim-bot/` container; `victim-bot/src/bot.js` (`adminBotCycle` / `processUrlQueue`, `managerBotCycle`, `staffBotCycle`). Observable over HTTP at `/platform/exploit-server/queue/pending`.
- What: First-hand live evidence this session. At 05:01:22 the pending queue held id=1 submitted **04:51:23** (~599s old). After a fresh 75s observation window, at 05:02:05 **both** id=1 (now ~674s / ~22 missed 30s cycles) and my freshly-submitted id=2 remained **PENDING** — nothing was ever marked visited. No reviews I submitted in the prior sess

## Verdict rationale (one line)
The dockerized lab is well-built and mostly solid, but the victim-bot URL queue does not process submitted URLs at runtime, which makes CS-02 and CS-03 unsolvable via their intended bot-delivery path — a gap that must close before convergence.

## What I read / ran / observed (claim enumeration)
- Read (source): node-app routes/manager.js, utils/jwt.js, middleware/auth.js, middleware/waf.js; victim-bot/src/bot.js and Dockerfile; flask-platform routes/exploit_server.py and routes/main.py; docker-compose.yml; garden/cli.py.

Verdict: refine

# Juice Garden — Whole-Build Gap/Robustness Review (F2)

## One-line
The platform, app, and challenge board are live and largely solid, but the **victim-bot performs none of its automated behaviors on the running stack** — a confirmed, deterministic CRITICAL failure that makes ~4 challenges unsolvable end-to-end. This alone bars `converged`. The fix is build/deploy-level (container rebuild + Playwright pinning), not architectural, so this is `refine`, not `operator_escalate`.

---

## CRITICAL — Victim-bot automation dead on the live stack (breaks INJ-01, CS-01, CS-02, CS-03)

**Evidence (my own live test, this session):**
- I submitted queue entry `id=1` (URL `http://garden.lab/health?revtest=…`) at `07:56:03`.
- At `08:03:16` — **433 s later (~14 missed 30 s processor cycles)** — `GET /platform/exploit-server/queue/pending` STILL returns that entry and `GET .../collections` is STILL `[]`.
- This extends my earlier same-stack observation (pending at 85 s) and the evaluator's REGRESSION (ai_verdicts idx 273, 13 consecutive sessions) into overwhelming, consistent evidence.

**Not a false positive — verified against the bot source:** `queueProcessorCycle` (victim-bot/src/bot.js L239-314) marks *every* entry visited regardless of URL, *including in the error path* (L300-304: a failed `page.goto` still POSTs `mark-visited`). There is no host allowlist. So an entry can only remain `pending` if the cycle never reaches its for-loop: timer not firing / process dead / stale container, or `createBrowser()`/login throws (L309-312). My probe URL would have been marked visited if the cycle ran at all.

**Scope of breakage:** the bot is the trigger for the lab's client-side/social challenges:
- INJ-01 (staff_bot 60 s report → second-order SQLi). Evaluator idx 123: `bio=null` after 84 s; manual `POST /api/staff/report` works → confirms the *mechanism* is fine but the *cycle does not auto-fire*.
- CS-01 (manager_bot/admin_bot review visit), CS-02 & CS-03 (URL-queue

# Whole-build review — Juice Garden (Docker vulnerable-web lab)

Gap/robustness review against operator intent (user_prompt.txt: "vulnerable box, web-based, multiple roles, moderate difficulty, contained in Docker so it can be reverted") and app_spec.txt (21 vulns / 23 flags across 6 tracks + MISSION-ALPHA + bonus, with a victim-bot driving the interactive/client-side challenges).

One CRITICAL gap blocks convergence: an entire advertised attack surface (Client-Side track + the second-order-SQLi trigger) cannot be solved end-to-end because the victim-bot never runs. The non-bot exploit surface, WAF, challenge board, and flag-distribution loop are solid.

---

## Critical gaps

### [G1] victim-bot never launches Chromium → Client-Side track (CS-01/02/03) and INJ-01 are unsolvable end-to-end

- **Where:** `victim-bot/Dockerfile` line 8 (`RUN npm install --omit=dev`) + `victim-bot/package.json` (`"playwright": "^1.40.0"`), with **no committed `package-lock.json`**, against base image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.40.0-jammy`. Launch site: `victim-bot/src/bot.js` `createBrowser()` (`chromium.launch(...)`), called inside `queueProcessorCycle` (~line 283) before the visit/mark-visited loop; outer catch (~line 309) swallows the throw without marking entries visited.
- **What:** With no lockfile, `npm install` resolves `^1.40.0` to the latest 1.x at build time, whose bundled-browser revision does **not** match the browsers baked into the `v1.40.0-jammy` base image. `chromium.launch()` then throws "Executable doesn't exist …". Because the throw happens before the visit loop, every bot cycle (staffBotCycle → INJ-01, managerBotCycle → review approval, adminBotCycle → CS-01, queueProcessorCycle → CS-02/CS-03) dies on entry and the exploit-server queue is never drained.
- **Why it matters (load-bearing):** The Client-Side track is advertised as a first-class category but is fully bot-gated — an attacker submits a URL/payload and a privileged bot must visit/ex

[... truncated]

VERDICT: refine

# Build Review — Juice Garden (Moderate-Difficulty Vulnerable Web Lab)

## Headline
The build is architecturally sound and most of its 21 challenges are wired correctly,
but a single committed defect in the victim-bot image makes **4 of the 21 challenges
(INJ-01, CS-01, CS-02, CS-03) unsolvable end-to-end**. The bot that is supposed to
trigger these vulnerabilities never launches a browser, so the flags can never be
produced by any player. This is a critical, load-bearing failure for a lab whose entire
purpose is that the flags be obtainable. It is fixable within the existing design (a
version pin / image alignment), so this is `refine`, not a blueprint problem.

## Verification surface exercised (this session + prior)
- CLI class: ran `garden hosts check` (deterministic output, MISSING/MISSING table, exit
  logic). Prior session also ran `garden --help`, `garden status`.
- UI class: `browser_navigate` to http://127.0.0.1/platform/ (live, "Challenge Board"),
  and `browser_evaluate` same-origin fetches: WAF blocks uppercase `UNION SELECT` (403),
  benign query passes (200), exploit-server queue read (200).
- Read cold: app_spec.txt (sliced), feature_list_digest.md, ai_verdicts.jsonl, README,
  UNVERIFIED.md, docker-compose.yml, victim-bot (Dockerfile/package.json/bot.js),
  node-app routes (products, staff, users, pages), waf.js, models seeding, flask
  exploit_server.py.
- NOT covered (residual, see below): full trace of auth.js, orders.js, graphql.js,
  webhooks.js, admin.js (MISSION-ALPHA gate), jwt.js, uuid_v1.js, flask challenges.py.

## CRITICAL — victim-bot Playwright browser never launches; 4 challenges unsolvable
Root cause is in committed build artifacts, not the environment:
- `victim-bot/Dockerfile` base image is `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.40.0-jammy`
  (Chromium baked for Playwright **1.40.0**).
- `victim-bot/package.json` pins `"playwright": "^1.40.0"` (caret), there is **no
  package-lock.json** (confirmed absent; the Dockerfile

VERDICT: refine

# F2 Whole-Build Review — Juice Garden (vulnerable lab box)

Fresh-context gap/robustness review. Question answered: *does the build deliver
what the spec promises, fully and solidly?* Architecture is NOT re-litigated
(it is sound — see below); this is a gap list driving the build toward solid.

## Verdict rationale

`refine` (not `converged`, not `operator_escalate`):

- **Not `converged`** — one CRITICAL defect makes 4 of 21 challenges
  deterministically unsolvable on a healthy stack (the *entire* Client-Side
  track CS-01/02/03 plus INJ-01). That is below the HOST-TOLERANCE FLOOR
  (17/21 < 18/21) AND independently trips the spec's DETERMINISTIC FAILURE
  EXCEPTION (a challenge that fails consistently on a healthy stack blocks
  "deployable" regardless of the floor). A critical finding bars `converged`.
- **Not `operator_escalate`** — the blueprint is correct. The victim-bot
  (Playwright headless Chromium driven off the exploit-server queue) is exactly
  the architecture the spec mandates, and all 21 vuln code paths match the spec.
  The blocker is a build/packaging defect (version pin + image rebuild +
  failure-masking), fixable by refinement without changing the design.

## Verification surface (this session)

- **UI (Playwright, live at http://127.0.0.1:80):** confirmed Garden Fresh home
  renders (nav, recent-deliveries table, product grid) and the Flask challenge
  board renders at `/platform/`. Exercised the exploit-server queue/collections
  API directly from the page origin (3 independent probes — see G1).
- **Static/control-flow trace (cold read):** all 12 node-app route files
  (`auth, users, orders, products, graphql, webhooks, staff, manager, admin,
  debug, challenges, pages`), `middleware/{auth,waf,csrf}.js`, `utils/jwt.js`,
  `utils/uuid_v1.js`, and `victim-bot/{Dockerfile,package.json,src/bot.js}`.
- **CLI (`garden`):** NOT exercisable here — Docker socket is sandbox-blocked in
  this review environment (matches the evaluator's

# Build Review — Juice Garden (Moderate-Difficulty Vulnerable Web Lab)

VERDICT: refine

## One-line
The architecture is sound and most challenge mechanics are present and (per code + sampled live checks) working, but a **deterministic dependency-pin defect breaks the victim-bot's Chromium cycles**, taking down the entire client-side (CS) track — CS-01, CS-02, CS-03 — and the failure is **silently masked** so the stack looks healthy. One critical, reproducible defect → not `converged`. The blueprint is correct and the fix is in-blueprint → not `operator_escalate`.

---

## Critical (blocks converged)

### C1 — victim-bot Chromium browser cycles fail on a clean build → CS-01 / CS-02 / CS-03 not solvable
Three independent evidence lines converge:

1. **Code (deterministic root cause).** `victim-bot/package.json` pins `"playwright": "^1.40.0"`. `victim-bot/Dockerfile` runs `npm install --omit=dev` with **no lockfile** (no `victim-bot/package-lock.json` exists; the Dockerfile comment explicitly chooses "lock-free builds"). The base image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.40.0-jammy` ships **only** the Chromium revision matching Playwright 1.40.0. The `^` caret floats `npm install` to the newest 1.x at build time, whose `chromium.launch()` expects a browser revision absent from the image → launch throws → `createBrowser()` (bot.js:92) fails.
2. **Live, this session.** I queued a beacon attack page via the exploit-server; the queue *drained* (entry marked visited) but `/collect` received **zero** hits from the bot, while a direct control request to `/collect` recorded instantly (source_ip 172.x). The bot never loaded the page.
3. **Live, this session — isolation.** The axios-only `managerBot` cycle **is alive**: a review I submitted (`approved:false`) auto-flipped to `approved:true` within one 30s cycle (count 1→2). So the bot *process* runs and the axios path works; **only the `createBrowser()` cycles fail.** This isolates the defect to Chromium launch, not the whole bot.

Whole-build gap review of Juice Garden (Docker-based moderate-difficulty vulnerable web lab; 275 features). This is `refine`, not a re-architecture — the overall blueprint is sound.

## What I checked (claim enumeration)

- **UI class (Playwright, live):** navigated to the served `board.html`. Shell renders with the spec design system — `#0D0F14` bg, `#E8A838` amber accent, `#38C988` mint; Syne heading, DM Sans body, JetBrains Mono flag input; full filter strip (All/Auth/Injection/Access Control/Client-Side/Server-Side/Business Logic/Mission/Moderate/Hard/Solved/Unsolved); slide-over panel with HINTS + SUBMIT FLAG + `JG{...}` placeholder. Console: only a benign `favicon.ico` 404 (no JS errors). NOTE: board.html is a server-rendered Jinja template; served statically the `{% for challenge in challenges %}` block shows literally (no Flask backend), so live dynamic rendering (cards/filter JS/solve animation) was NOT verifiable here and rests on the EVALUATOR record (features 91-96, 113-123 CONFIRMED_PASSING).
- **Traced (control-flow / enforcement):** `utils/jwt.js` — AUTH-01 `verifyKidHmac` does a real `fs.readFileSync(kidPath)` (kid=/dev/null → empty key), AUTH-02 `verifyAlgFlexible` does real RS256→HS256 raw-HMAC confusion; alg=none rejected in both. `middleware/waf.js` — real regex enforcement; intentional bypasses correctly built (

### Findings emitted by this reviewer

#### [G1] Concurrent INJ-01 payloads cannot both fire — the first-iterated attacker's `--` silences every later payload in the single-line report query — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `node-app/src/routes/staff.js` lines 106-119 (the `customers.map(...).join(' UNION ALL ')` single-line assembly + `sequelize.query(rawReportSql)`); spec claim in `app_spec.txt` `<tenancy_model>` lines 42-48; feature 55 Step 3.
  **What:** Every customer username is concatenated into ONE single-line SQL statement. The documented exploit payload (staff.js lines 78-79) ends with `... SELECT 1 --`. SQL `--` comments to end-of-line, and the whole statement is one line, so the FIRST attacker fragment's `--` comments out the closing paren, the main SELECT, AND every subsequent fragment — including a second attacker's `update_op` UPDATE. Trace with customers `[alice, attacker1, attacker2]`: only `attacker1`'s UPDATE executes; `attacker2`'s is inside the commented-out tail and never runs. Because attacker1's row persists, attacker2 is permanently locked out (silent no-op, bio stays null) until `garden reset`. The spec's `<tenancy_model>` explicitly promises the opposite: "independent
```

#### [G2] One malformed/quote-bearing username bricks the entire staff report for all users until reset — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `node-app/src/routes/staff.js` lines 106-119 (same single-query assembly).
  **What:** Because ALL usernames go into one raw query, any single username with a stray `;`, unbalanced quote, or even a legit apostrophe (e.g. `O'Brien`) raises a PostgreSQL syntax error that aborts the whole statement — so NO attacker's bio updates that cycle (and every cycle after, since the bad row persists). This is the actual mechanism behind the EVALUATOR's "DB contamination" that downgraded features 52/55/133/254 to PARTIAL.
  **Why:** Fragile shared state: a single wrong attempt (or one odd username) silently disables INJ-01 for everyone until `garden reset`, with no operator-visible signal beyond a 500 in bot logs. Recoverable via reset, but harsh for a lab meant to tolerate experimentation.
  **Fix shape:** Same per-username isolation as
```

#### [G1] (per-row try/catch around each fragment's execution) bounds the blast radius so one bad username degrades only that row, not the whole report. — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
-
```

#### [G3] `UNVERIFIED.md` misattributes the INJ-01 PARTIALs to recoverable "test contamination" — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `UNVERIFIED.md` feature 52/55/133/254 entries ("On clean stack ... bio update would complete" / "would work on a clean stack").
  **What:** For feature 55 specifically the root cause is structural (
```

#### [G1] ), not session contamination — it cannot pass on any clean stack as written. The doc's framing implies a re-run will clear it, which will instead loop it as PARTIAL indefinitely without a code fix. — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Why:** Doc accuracy: misleads the next session/operator into expecting auto-resolution of a defect that needs a code change. (Features 52/133/254 single-attacker WILL pass on a clean stack — those entries are accurate; only the feature-55 concurrent claim is wrong.)
  **Fix shape:** After fixing
```

#### [G1] , update the feature-55 note to reflect the structural cause/fix; leave the single-attacker entries as-is. — severity: minor

# F2 Whole-Build Gap/Robustness Review — Juice Garden (Moderate-Difficulty Vulnerable Web Lab)

promise for at least one vulnerability track, plus a live stability observation.

---

## VERDICT RATIONALE (short)

`refine`, not `converged`, because at least one **critical functional gap** is present and
independently corroborated: the entire **client_side track (CS-01/02/03)** is non-functional
because the victim-bot's Playwright cycles do not actually navigate — the work queue is drained
through an error-path that marks entries "visited" without ever loading them. An XSS-on-admin lab
whose admin victim never renders the page cannot deliver its client-side flags by the intended
path. This is confirmed both by the evaluator's own regression verdicts (session s34, features
71/74/77/273) and by reading `victim-bot/src/bot.js` directly.

Not `operator_escalate`: the blueprint is not wrong. These are fixable implementation defects in

### Findings emitted by this reviewer

#### [G1] Entire Client-Side track (CS-01/CS-02/CS-03) is non-functional — the Playwright victim-bot cycles never perform a real navigation, and the failure is masked by an error-path that marks queue entries "visited" anyway — severity: critical

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `victim-bot/src/bot.js` — `adminBotCycle` (lines 166-197) and `queueProcessorCycle`/`_queueProcessorCycleInner` (204-287); specifically the per-entry catch (262-271) and outer browser-failure catch (273-283), both of which POST `mark-visited` regardless of whether `page.goto` ever succeeded. Healthcheck-masking in `docker-compose.yml:213` (see G2).
  **What:** I dispatched a self-evidencing collect URL through the live exploit-server Bot Dispatch. After ~35s the queue drained (pending=0) but the Collected Data table stayed empty; a direct same-origin GET to the identical collect endpoint recorded a row immediately. So the bot marked the entry visited without its Chromium navigating — independently reproducing ai_verdicts REGRESSION s34 for idx 71/74/77/132/256/257/258/273. All three CS challenges depend on these Playwright cycles (CS-01: admin_bot visits `/reviews`; CS-02/CS-03: queue processor visits the attack page). The axios-only cycles (staff_bot→INJ-01, manager_bot→app
```

#### [G2] victim-bot healthcheck is process-existence only — reports "healthy" while 2 of 4 cycles are dead — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `docker-compose.yml:213` — `test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pgrep -f 'node.*bot' > /dev/null || exit 1"]`.
  **What:** The healthcheck passes whenever the node process is alive; it never verifies Chromium can launch or that the admin/queue cycles perform real visits. So feature 6 ("Victim bot shows healthy within 30s") and `garden status` will report victim-bot HEALTHY even though the entire CS track is non-functional.
  **Why:** This is the orchestration-level half of G1's masking: an operator runs `garden status`, sees a green victim-bot, and concludes CS-01/02/03 work. The check asserts the process exists, not the behavior it is supposed to perform — the exact "flag set ≠ behavior gated" anti-pattern.
  **Fix shape:** Drive the healthcheck from actual cycle success — have the bot record a last-successful-navigation timestamp and fail health if no real queue/admin visit has completed within N intervals (or fail if the most recent `chromium.launch()` failed).
-
```

#### [G3] `UNVERIFIED.md` misrepresents readiness — it omits all 8 confirmed-broken REGRESSION features — severity: major

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `UNVERIFIED.md` (whole file). It lists only the 8 Docker-blocked UNVERIFIABLE features (1,2,6,121,123,156,200,272) under "env-limited caveats … (not failing)"; its header promises a "PARTIAL — downgraded" section that does not exist.
  **What:** The 8 REGRESSION features (71,74,77,132,256,257,258,273 — the entire CS track + bot Playwright health, per ai_verdicts s34) appear nowhere in the shipped operator docs, while README advertises "21 deliberately introduced vulnerabilities" and the board renders all 21 as if solvable.
  **Why:** An operator reading the shipped docs would believe everything not listed in `UNVERIFIED.md` passes and would deploy expecting CS-01/02/03 to work. This is the inverse docs-accuracy gap: user-facing documentation that misdescribes the true state. (I treat `UNVERIFIED.md` as contractor work product, not operator direction.)
  **Fix shape:** After G1/G2 are fixed and the bot is verified navigating, this largely resolves. Until then the doc must lis
```

#### [G4] Deployability (#1 success criterion) and 7 sibling Docker-lifecycle features are unverifiable in this sandbox; correctness rests on unexecuted orchestration — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** `garden/cli.py` start/stop/reset/status + `docker-compose.yml`; features 1,2,6,121,123,156,200,272 (UNVERIFIABLE in ai_verdicts).
  **What:** The Docker socket is sandbox-blocked, so `garden start`/`status`/`reset` and health-check timing could not be executed. `docker-compose.yml` (8 services, two `internal:true` networks, `depends_on`/`service_completed_successfully`, healthchecks) and the CLI read as well-formed, but the deploy path itself is unexecuted here.
  **Why:** Deployability is the spec's first success criterion and cannot be confirmed in this environment. This is a verification limitation, not a proven defect — but note that fixing G2 is precisely what would let a real `garden status` reveal G1 on deploy.
  **Fix shape:** Verify on a real Docker host (Engine 24+, 4GB). No code change implied beyond G1/G2.
-
```

#### [G5] `/favicon.ico` 404 on Garden Fresh pages — severity: minor

**Specific issue / Why this matters:**

```
**Where:** Garden Fresh HTML/static serving (node-app) — no favicon route.
  **What:** Every Garden Fresh page logs one console error: "Failed to load resource: 404 (Not Found) @ /favicon.ico".
  **Why:** Cosmetic only; the app is meant to "present as a commercial product" (feature 126), so a 404 on every load is a small polish gap. Not functionally harmful.
  **Fix shape:** Serve a favicon or add an inline `<link rel="icon">` to suppress the request.
```

VERDICT: refine

# Build review — Juice Garden (whole-build, fresh context)

Scope: gap/robustness review against the brief (Docker-contained, deliberately-vulnerable, web-based, multi-role, moderate-difficulty, lab-based, fully contained/reversible) and app_spec.txt. Architecture (F1) is not re-litigated. One blocking gap found ⇒ refine.

The build is largely solid: the spec is faithfully realised across most of the 21 challenges, the `garden` CLI is fully implemented (not stubbed), and the non-bot attack tracks I exercised live all behave as specified. The blocker is that one entire challenge track (Client-Side, 3 of 21 challenges) is dead end-to-end due to a deterministic build defect in the victim-bot image, which the spec's DETERMINISTIC FAILURE EXCEPTION says must be fixed before the build is deployable.

---

## [G1] (CRITICAL) Client-Side track (CS-01/02/03) is non-functional end-to-end — deterministic build defect

**Where:** `victim-bot/Dockerfile` + `victim-bot/package.json` + (absent) `victim-bot/package-lock.json`; manifests in `victim-bot/src/bot.js` `adminBotCycle` and `queueProcessorCycle`.

**What:** The image is built `FROM mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.40.0-jammy` (browser binaries pinned to Playwright 1.40.0), but `package.json` declares `"playwright": "^1.40.0"` and the Dockerfile installs it with `RUN npm install --omit=dev` (the Dockerfile comment even states "npm ci requires a package-lock.json; use npm install for lock-free builds"). I confirmed there is no lockfile in `victim-bot/` (only `src/bot.js`, `src/.keep`, `package.json`, `Dockerfile`). With no pin, `npm install` resolves `^1.40.0` to the newest 1.x at build time, whose bundled Chromium revision is NOT the one pre-installed in the v1.40.0 image. `chromium.launch()` then throws ("browser executable doesn't exist"). The result: the two Playwright-driven cycles (adminBotCycle → CS-01 admin visit; queueProcessorCycle → CS-02/CS-03 exploit-server queue) perform 0 real navigations, whil

[... truncated]

- **BL-01** negative-quantity and **INJ-03** EJS SSTI → real flags (earlier in session).

This was NOT an exhaustive live exploitation of all 21 challenges. My evidence is the union of:

Reading `ai_verdicts.jsonl` was used only as corroboration, not as a substitute for live checks.

- **BAC-03 discovery is real:** GraphQL introspection (200) exposes `Order.adminNotes`. [browser_evaluate]
- `/api/debug/stats` (hidden bonus) → 401 auth-gated, not openly exposed. [browser_evaluate]
